## 3 PanoCARLA Dataset

T<sub>o a</sub>dd<sub>ress</sub> th<sub>e scarc</sub>it<sub>y o</sub>f RGB<sub>-</sub>D <sub>panoram</sub>i<sub>c v</sub>id<sub>eo</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a,</sub> <sub>we</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uce</sub> P<sub>a</sub>n<sub>o</sub>CARLA<sub>,</sub> <sub>a</sub> l<sub>arge-sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>syn</sub>th<sub>e</sub>ti<sub>c</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t curated within CARLA simulator (Dosovitski<sub>y</sub> et al., 2017). While existing approaches often synthesize trajectories b<sub>y</sub> inter<sub>p</sub>olatin<sub>g</sub> between discrete wa<sub>yp</sub>oints (Lu et al., 2024), we ca<sub>p</sub>ture more natural motion b<sub>y</sub> mountin<sub>g</sub> <sub>cameras</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> <sub>on</sub>t<sub>o</sub> <sub>au</sub>t<sub>onomous</sub> <sub>ve</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>l<sub>es</sub> t<sub>o</sub> l<sub>everage</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>mu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>or</sub>’<sub>s</sub> b<sub>u</sub>ilt<sub>-</sub>i<sub>n</sub> <sub>nav</sub>i<sub>ga</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>po</sub>li<sub>c</sub>i<sub>es.</sub>

H<sub>owever,</sub> thi<sub>s se</sub>t<sub>up</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uces a cr</sub>iti<sub>ca</sub>l i<sub>ssue:</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>ve</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>l<sub>e</sub> b<sub>o</sub>d<sub>y</sub> i<sub>nev</sub>it<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>y causes pers</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t <sub>se</sub>lf<sub>-occ</sub>l<sub>us</sub>i<sub>on</sub> (see Fig. 5). To resolve this, we design a Two-Stage Decoupled Data Acquisition Pipeline, as illustrated in Fig. 2. The pipeline separates trajectory acquisition from <sub>scene</sub> <sub>ren</sub>d<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ng:</sub>

Stage I: Natural Trajectory Planning. We generate <sub>smoo</sub>th d<sub>r</sub>i<sub>v</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>pa</sub>th<sub>s</sub> b<sub>y</sub> d<sub>es</sub>i<sub>gna</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> <sub>sparse</sub> <sub>waypo</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>an</sub>d l<sub>everag</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> CARLA’<sub>s</sub> A<sub>u</sub>t<sub>op</sub>il<sub>o</sub>t f<sub>or</sub> <sub>p</sub>h<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t <sub>nav</sub>i<sub>ga</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> W<sub>e recor</sub>d th<sub>e resu</sub>lti<sub>ng</sub> 6<sub>-</sub>D<sub>o</sub>F <sub>ve</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>l<sub>e poses</sub> <sub>a</sub>t 20 FPS<sub>,</sub> i<sub>ncorpora</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> h<sub>uman-</sub>i<sub>n-</sub>th<sub>e-</sub>l<sub>oop</sub> <sub>re</sub>fi<sub>nemen</sub>t to iteratively adjust waypoints for optimal smoothness.

![](images/68383901ac20ad8aab0e79a18495b97d2ad9cb96aa191b8b193595b0191be777.jpg)  
Figure 3. Overview of the proposed PVDepth. Building upon a pre-trained video difusion backbone, PVDepth incorporates two key spatiotemporal adaptations to bridge the geometric gap between perspective and panoramic domains: (1) Progressive Sphere-aware Noise Initialization, which smoothly transitions the input noise from a planar to a spherical distribution to adapt to non-uniform ERP information density; and (2) Cube-rectified Temporal Modeling, which introduces an auxiliary distortion-free Cubemap branch to <sub>cap</sub>t<sub>ure</sub> li<sub>near mo</sub>ti<sub>on mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>li<sub>ng, rec</sub>tif<sub>y</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> ERP<sub>-</sub>i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>uce</sub>d <sub>non-</sub>li<sub>near</sub> d<sub>ynam</sub>i<sub>cs</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e pr</sub>i<sub>mary</sub> ERP b<sub>ranc</sub>h<sub>.</sub>

## 5.4 Limitations

Whil<sub>e</sub> PVD<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>ac</sub>hi<sub>eves super</sub>i<sub>or per</sub>f<sub>ormance</sub> i<sub>n</sub> <sub>panoram</sub>i<sub>c v</sub>id<sub>eo</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>es</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>ti<sub>on, we ac</sub>k<sub>now</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d<sub>ge</sub> <sub>cer</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub> li<sub>m</sub>it<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons regar</sub>di<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence e</sub>fi<sub>c</sub>i<sub>ency.</sub> A<sub>s</sub> <sub>s</sub>h<sub>own</sub> i<sub>n</sub> T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 8<sub>,</sub> <sub>we</sub> <sub>repor</sub>t i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ency,</sub> GPU <sub>memory,</sub> FLOP<sub>s, an</sub>d <sub>accuracy</sub> f<sub>or</sub> dif<sub>eren</sub>t <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s a</sub>t 1024×512 resolution usin<sub>g</sub> 110-frame videos. Com<sub>p</sub>ared with the baseline De<sub>p</sub>thCrafter (Hu et al., 2025), PVDe<sub>p</sub>th <sub>ac</sub>hi<sub>eves</sub> b<sub>e</sub>tt<sub>er</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>accuracy w</sub>ith <sub>near</sub>l<sub>y</sub> id<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>l GPU <sub>memory usage,</sub> b<sub>u</sub>t i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uces</sub> hi<sub>g</sub>h<sub>er</sub> FLOP<sub>s an</sub>d l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ency</sub> mainly due to the bi-directional projection between ERP <sub>an</sub>d C<sub>u</sub>b<sub>e</sub> f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ures.</sub> F<sub>u</sub>t<sub>ure wor</sub>k <sub>cou</sub>ld <sub>a</sub>dd<sub>ress</sub> thi<sub>s</sub> b<sub>y</sub> exploring more eficient projection algorithms.

Also, our method is slower than UniK3D (Piccinelli et al., 2025). ## A.2 Stage II: Unobstructed RGB-D Data Recording

This phase aims to render clean scene data using the recorded trajectories while introducing vertical motion complexity. We construct a virtual rigid rig of six synchronized RGB-D cameras. Raw simulator out<sub>p</sub>ut is <sub>p</sub>lanar Z-de<sub>p</sub>th (<sub>p</sub>rojected alon<sub>g</sub> the o<sub>p</sub>tical axis), which is <sub>g</sub>eometricall<sub>y</sub> i<sub>ncompa</sub>tibl<sub>e w</sub>ith <sub>sp</sub>h<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>s</sub>tit<sub>c</sub>hi<sub>ng</sub> d<sub>ue</sub> t<sub>o</sub> di<sub>vergen</sub>t <sub>op</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>axes.</sub> Di<sub>rec</sub>t <sub>s</sub>tit<sub>c</sub>hi<sub>ng wou</sub>ld <sub>cause severe ar</sub>tif<sub>ac</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> Therefore, we im<sub>p</sub>lement a <sub>p</sub>ixel-wise calibration ste<sub>p</sub>: convertin<sub>g</sub> Z-de<sub>p</sub>th to Euclidean distance (radial distance from the o<sub>p</sub>tical center) before stitchin<sub>g</sub>. This ensures that de<sub>p</sub>th values are ali<sub>g</sub>ned to a unified s<sub>p</sub>herical metric.

## B Preliminaries: SVD and DepthCrafter

Our framework is built u on De thCrafter (Hu et al., 2025), which re ur oses the architecture of Stable Video Difusion (SVD) (Blattmann et al., 2023) for hi<sub>g</sub>h-qualit<sub>y</sub> video de<sub>p</sub>th estimation. In this section, we first review the formulation <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e un</sub>d<sub>er</sub>l<sub>y</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> SVD <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>an</sub>d th<sub>en</sub> d<sub>escr</sub>ib<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e spec</sub>ifi<sub>c a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uce</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> D<sub>ep</sub>thC<sub>ra</sub>ft<sub>er.</sub>

Stable Video Difusion (SVD). SVD is a latent video difusion model that o<sub>p</sub>erates in a com<sub>p</sub>ressed s<sub>p</sub>ace learned b<sub>y</sub> a Variational Autoencoder (VAE) to reduce com<sub>p</sub>utational com<sub>p</sub>lexit<sub>y</sub>. Given a data sam<sub>p</sub>le x (e.<sub>g</sub>., a video cli<sub>p</sub>), the VAE encoder E ma s it to a latent re resentation ${ \bf z } _ { 0 } = \mathcal { E } ( { \bf x } )$ <sub>.</sub> D<sub>ur</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng, a</sub> f<sub>orwar</sub>d <sub>process progress</sub>i<sub>ve</sub>l<sub>y corrup</sub>t<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ean</sub> l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t $\mathbf { z } _ { 0 }$ <sub>w</sub>ith G<sub>auss</sub>i<sub>an no</sub>i<sub>se</sub> $\epsilon \sim \mathcal { N } ( \mathbf { 0 } , \mathbf { I } )$ <sub>.</sub> At ti<sub>me s</sub>t<sub>ep</sub> �<sub>,</sub> th<sub>e no</sub>i<sub>sy</sub> l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t $\mathbf { z } _ { t }$ i<sub>s</sub> f<sub>ormu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as:</sub>

$$
{ \bf z } _ { t } = { \bf z } _ { 0 } + \sigma _ { t } \epsilon ,\tag{5}
$$

<sub>w</sub>h<sub>ere</sub> $\sigma _ { t }$ <sub>con</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>l<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>no</sub>i<sub>se</sub> l<sub>eve</sub>l<sub>.</sub> A d<sub>eno</sub>i<sub>ser</sub> <sub>ne</sub>t<sub>wor</sub>k $D _ { \theta }$ i<sub>s</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> <sub>recover</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ean</sub> $\mathbf { z } _ { 0 }$ f<sub>rom</sub> $\mathbf { z } _ { t }$ <sub>,</sub> <sub>con</sub>diti<sub>one</sub>d <sub>on</sub> context c. The training objective minimizes the weighted squared error:

$$
\mathcal { L } = \mathbb { E } _ { \mathbf { z } _ { 0 } , \mathbf { z } _ { t } , t , \mathbf { c } } \left[ w ( \sigma _ { t } ) \lVert D _ { \theta } ( \mathbf { z } _ { t } ; \boldsymbol { \sigma } _ { t } , \mathbf { c } ) - \mathbf { z } _ { 0 } \rVert _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } \right] ,\tag{6}
$$

Ad<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n in D<sub>ep</sub>thCr<sub>a</sub>ft<sub>e</sub>r<sub>.</sub> Si<sub>nce</sub> SVD i<sub>s</sub> <sub>or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> d<sub>es</sub>i<sub>gne</sub>d f<sub>or</sub> i<sub>mage-</sub>t<sub>o-v</sub>id<sub>eo</sub> <sub>genera</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> D<sub>ep</sub>thC<sub>ra</sub>ft<sub>er</sub> i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uces</sub> t<sub>wo</sub> k<sub>ey mo</sub>difi<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> t<sub>o a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t it f<sub>or v</sub>id<sub>eo-</sub>t<sub>o-</sub>d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>es</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>ti<sub>on:</sub>

• Latent S<sub>p</sub>ace Ada<sub>p</sub>tation. The <sub>p</sub>re-trained VAE in SVD ex<sub>p</sub>ects 3-channel RGB in<sub>p</sub>uts. To <sub>p</sub>rocess sin<sub>g</sub>le-channel d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>maps</sub> d<sub>,</sub> D<sub>ep</sub>thC<sub>ra</sub>ft<sub>er rep</sub>li<sub>ca</sub>t<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>map</sub> th<sub>ree</sub> ti<sub>mes</sub> b<sub>e</sub>f<sub>ore enco</sub>di<sub>ng:</sub> $\mathbf { z } ^ { ( d ) } = \mathcal { E } ( \mathbf { d } )$ <sub>.</sub> D<sub>ur</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> d<sub>eco</sub>di<sub>ng,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> th<sub>ree ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t <sub>c</sub>h<sub>anne</sub>l<sub>s</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub> VAE d<sub>eco</sub>d<sub>er are average</sub>d t<sub>o recons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e-c</sub>h<sub>anne</sub>l d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>sequence.</sub>

• Video Conditionin<sub>g</sub>. To ensure the <sub>g</sub>enerated de<sub>p</sub>th is strictl<sub>y</sub> ali<sub>g</sub>ned with the in<sub>p</sub>ut video<sub>,</sub> De<sub>p</sub>thCrafter modifies the conditionin<sub>g</sub> mechanism. Instead of conditionin<sub>g</sub> on a sin<sub>g</sub>le ima<sub>g</sub>e (as in the ori<sub>g</sub>inal SVD), it conditions on the <sub>en</sub>ti<sub>re</sub> i<sub>npu</sub>t <sub>v</sub>id<sub>eo sequence v.</sub> S<sub>pec</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y,</sub> th<sub>e v</sub>id<sub>eo</sub> l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t $\mathbf { z } ^ { ( \nu ) } = \mathcal { E } ( \mathbf { v } )$ i<sub>s conca</sub>t<sub>ena</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>rame-w</sub>i<sub>se w</sub>ith th<sub>e no</sub>i<sub>sy</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t $\mathbf { z } _ { t }$ <sub>a</sub>l<sub>ong</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>c</sub>h<sub>anne</sub>l di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>on.</sub> Additi<sub>ona</sub>ll<sub>y,</sub> CLIP <sub>em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ngs</sub> <sub>ex</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>rom</sub> <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>v</sub>id<sub>eo</sub> f<sub>rame</sub> <sub>are</sub> injected via cross-attention to provide semantic guidance.

## D Implementation Details

Training details of PVDepth. Our PVDe<sub>p</sub>th is initialized with <sub>p</sub>re-trained De<sub>p</sub>thCrafter (Hu et al., 2025) wei<sub>g</sub>hts. To ensure stable conver<sub>g</sub>ence, we em<sub>p</sub>lo<sub>y</sub> a two-sta<sub>g</sub>e trainin<sub>g</sub> strate<sub>gy</sub>: Sta<sub>g</sub>e I (S<sub>p</sub>atial Ada<sub>p</sub>tation): We fine-tune the s<sub>p</sub>atial la<sub>y</sub>ers for 20k ste<sub>p</sub>s usin<sub>g</sub> our Pro<sub>g</sub>ressive S<sub>p</sub>here-aware Noise Initialization (PSNI). This sta<sub>g</sub>e focuses on ada<sub>p</sub>tin<sub>g</sub> the U-Net s<sub>p</sub>atial <sub>p</sub>riors to the ERP’s s<sub>p</sub>herical <sub>g</sub>eometr<sub>y</sub>. Sta<sub>g</sub>e II (Tem<sub>p</sub>oral Rectification): We introduce the Cube-rectified Temporal Modeling module and fine-tune only the newly added projection layers for another 20k steps. Cruciall<sub>y</sub>, durin<sub>g</sub> this sta<sub>g</sub>e, the noise initialization is fixed to the final state of PSNI (i.e., usin<sub>g</sub> the constant residual <sub>p</sub>l<sub>anar we</sub>i<sub>g</sub>ht $w _ { f i n a l }$ without further annealin<sub>g</sub>).

## E Evaluation Metrics

Followin<sub>g</sub> evaluation <sub>p</sub>rotocols in <sub>p</sub>revious video de<sub>p</sub>th estimation works (Hu et al., 2025; Chen et al., 2025; Yan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025a), we em<sub>p</sub>lo<sub>y</sub> metrics includin<sub>g</sub> Absolute Relative Error (AbsRel) and Threshold Accurac<sub>y</sub> $( \delta _ { 1 } )$ <sub>.</sub> L<sub>e</sub>t $d _ { i }$ <sub>an</sub>d $\hat { d } _ { i }$ d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e roun</sub>d t<sub>ru</sub>th <sub>an</sub>d <sub>re</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d d<sub>e</sub> th <sub>va</sub>l<sub>ues a</sub>t <sub>va</sub>lid i<sub>xe</sub>l �<sub>, an</sub>d � b<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>o</sub>t<sub>a</sub>l <sub>num</sub>b<sub>er o</sub>f <sub>va</sub>lid i<sub>xe</sub>l<sub>s.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> Ab<sub>s</sub>R<sub>e</sub>l <sub>me</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> i<sub>s</sub> f<sub>ormu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as:</sub>

$$
\mathrm { A b s R e l } = \frac { 1 } { N } \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { N } \frac { | d _ { i } - \hat { d } _ { i } | } { d _ { i } } .\tag{8}
$$

Th<sub>e</sub> th<sub>res</sub>h<sub>o</sub>ld <sub>accuracy</sub> $\delta _ { 1 }$ i<sub>s</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>as:</sub>

$$
\delta _ { 1 } = \frac { 1 } { N } \sum _ { i = 1 } ^ { N } \mathbb { I } \left( \operatorname* { m a x } \left( \frac { d _ { i } } { \hat { d } _ { i } } , \frac { \hat { d } _ { i } } { d _ { i } } \right) < 1 . 2 5 \right) .\tag{9}
$$

where I(·) is the indicator function. Consistent with normal outdoor settin<sub>g</sub>s (Hu et al., 2025), we ca<sub>p</sub> the maximum <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>a</sub>t 80<sub>m.</sub>

In addition, followin<sub>g</sub> (Hu et al., 2025; Chen et al., 2025; Yan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025a), we <sub>p</sub>erform afine-invariant alignment t<sub>o e</sub>li<sub>m</sub>i<sub>na</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e sca</sub>l<sub>e an</sub>d <sub>s</sub>hift <sub>am</sub>bi<sub>gu</sub>it<sub>y</sub> i<sub>n</sub>h<sub>eren</sub>t i<sub>n monocu</sub>l<sub>ar</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>es</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> S<sub>pec</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y, we a</sub>li<sub>gn</sub> th<sub>e pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d d<sub>ep</sub>th t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>groun</sub>d t<sub>ru</sub>th b<sub>y</sub> <sub>op</sub>ti<sub>m</sub>i<sub>z</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>�</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>s</sub>hift �<sub>.</sub> C<sub>ruc</sub>i<sub>a</sub>ll<sub>y,</sub> t<sub>o</sub> f<sub>a</sub>ithf<sub>u</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> <sub>re</sub>fl<sub>ec</sub>t t<sub>empora</sub>l <sub>cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ency,</sub> <sub>we</sub> perform this alignment globally, optimizing a single pair of (�, �) for the entire video clip. This stands in contrast to per-frame alignment, where independent adjustments for each frame can artificially conceal temporal flickering artifacts.

## F Quantitative Reprojection-based Geometric Consistency Evaluation

D<sub>ue</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>ac</sub>k <sub>o</sub>f <sub>pu</sub>bli<sub>c</sub>l<sub>y ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e rea</sub>l<sub>-wor</sub>ld <sub>panoram</sub>i<sub>c v</sub>id<sub>eo</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t<sub>s w</sub>ith d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>anno</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons, we canno</sub>t di<sub>rec</sub>tl<sub>y</sub> evaluate the accuracy of predicted depth in real-world scenarios. Therefore, this section introduces a reprojection-based <sub>geome</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ency eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l f<sub>or quan</sub>tit<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub>l<sub>y assess</sub>i<sub>ng ex</sub>i<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>ng me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s un</sub>d<sub>er rea</sub>l<sub>-wor</sub>ld <sub>con</sub>diti<sub>ons.</sub> All ex<sub>p</sub>eriments are conducted on GenEx Real-World, a real <sub>p</sub>anoramic video subset of GenEx-DB (Lu et al., 2024).

This dataset is well suited for reprojection-based evaluation, as its scenes are predominantly static campus environments, <sub>sa</sub>ti<sub>s</sub>f<sub>y</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>a</sub> k<sub>ey</sub> <sub>assump</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>requ</sub>i<sub>re</sub>d f<sub>or</sub> <sub>geome</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> <sub>cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ency</sub> <sub>ana</sub>l<sub>ys</sub>i<sub>s.</sub>

## F.1 Experimental Setup

W<sub>e ran</sub>d<sub>om</sub>l<sub>y se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t 50 <sub>v</sub>id<sub>eo c</sub>li<sub>ps</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> th<sub>e</sub> G<sub>en</sub>E<sub>x</sub> R<sub>ea</sub>l<sub>-</sub>W<sub>or</sub>ld d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t f<sub>or eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Si<sub>nce</sub> G<sub>en</sub>E<sub>x</sub> R<sub>ea</sub>l<sub>-</sub>W<sub>or</sub>ld d<sub>oes</sub> not provide ground-truth camera poses for each frame, we estimate the camera trajectory for each clip using the SLAM module <sub>p</sub>rovided in ViPE (Huan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025) (see A<sub>pp</sub>endix G). The estimated <sub>p</sub>oses are treated as reference <sub>p</sub>oses and are used solely for geometric reprojection during evaluation.

Although the estimated camera poses may not be perfectly accurate, ViPE jointly optimizes camera poses and depth <sub>es</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>t<sub>es, resu</sub>lti<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>n a se</sub>lf<sub>-cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t d<sub>ep</sub>th<sub>–pose con</sub>fi<sub>gura</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> W<sub>e</sub> th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore a</sub>li<sub>gn</sub> th<sub>e pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d d<sub>ep</sub>th<sub>s o</sub>f <sub>a</sub>ll <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f ViPE t<sub>o reso</sub>l<sub>ve sca</sub>l<sub>e am</sub>bi<sub>gu</sub>it<sub>y across</sub> dif<sub>eren</sub>t d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s an</sub>d <sub>ensure a</sub> f<sub>a</sub>i<sub>r</sub> <sub>compar</sub>i<sub>son.</sub> S<sub>pec</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ll<sub>y,</sub> f<sub>or eac</sub>h <sub>v</sub>id<sub>eo c</sub>li<sub>p, a s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e g</sub>l<sub>o</sub>b<sub>a</sub>l <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>e an</sub>d <sub>s</sub>hift i<sub>s es</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>over</sub> th<sub>e en</sub>ti<sub>re sequence an</sub>d <sub>app</sub>li<sub>e</sub>d <sub>un</sub>if<sub>orm</sub>l<sub>y</sub> t<sub>o a</sub>ll f<sub>rames,</sub> f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>ow</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e same pro</sub>t<sub>oco</sub>l d<sub>escr</sub>ib<sub>e</sub>d i<sub>n</sub> A<sub>ppen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> E<sub>.</sub> Thi<sub>s v</sub>id<sub>eo-</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>l <sub>a</sub>li<sub>gnmen</sub>t i<sub>mp</sub>li<sub>c</sub>itl<sub>y requ</sub>i<sub>res</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> t<sub>o rema</sub>i<sub>n accura</sub>t<sub>e an</sub>d t<sub>empora</sub>ll<sub>y cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t <sub>across</sub> f<sub>rames.</sub>

F<sub>or</sub> <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>v</sub>id<sub>eo</sub> <sub>c</sub>li<sub>p,</sub> <sub>we</sub> <sub>se</sub>l<sub>ec</sub>t <sub>mu</sub>lti<sub>p</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>anc</sub>h<sub>or</sub> f<sub>rames</sub> <sub>a</sub>t fi<sub>xe</sub>d t<sub>empora</sub>l i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erva</sub>l<sub>s,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d f<sub>or</sub> <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>anc</sub>h<sub>or</sub> f<sub>rame,</sub> <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> predicted depth of that frame is used to back-project the panoramic image into a 3D point cloud. Using the estimated camera poses from ViPE, the point cloud is then forward-projected to subsequent panoramic frames. ## F.2 Evaluation Metric

L<sub>e</sub>t $I _ { t } \in \mathbb { R } ^ { H \times W \times 3 }$ d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e rea</sub>l t<sub>arge</sub>t <sub>panoram</sub>i<sub>c</sub> i<sub>mage a</sub>t ti<sub>me</sub> �<sub>, an</sub>d l<sub>e</sub>t $\hat { I } _ { t }$ d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e syn</sub>th<sub>es</sub>i<sub>ze</sub>d i<sub>mage o</sub>bt<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>v</sub>i<sub>a</sub> reprojection. Let $M _ { t } \in \{ 0 , 1 \} ^ { H \times W }$ b<sub>e a</sub> bi<sub>nary mas</sub>k i<sub>n</sub>di<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>ng va</sub>lid <sub>p</sub>i<sub>xe</sub>l<sub>s</sub> f<sub>or eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on, an</sub>d d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub> th<sub>e se</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f <sub>va</sub>lid <sub>p</sub>i<sub>xe</sub>l<sub>s</sub> <sub>as</sub> $\Omega = \{ i \mid M _ { t } ( i ) = 1 \}$

We use the Structural Similarit<sub>y</sub> Index (SSIM) as the evaluation metric to measure re<sub>p</sub>rojection-based <sub>g</sub>eometric <sub>cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ency.</sub> SSIM <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>es percep</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>l <sub>s</sub>i<sub>m</sub>il<sub>ar</sub>it<sub>y</sub> b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween</sub> t<sub>wo</sub> i<sub>mages</sub> b<sub>y compar</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> l<sub>um</sub>i<sub>nance, con</sub>t<sub>ras</sub>t<sub>, an</sub>d <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ura</sub>l i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>orma</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> Gi<sub>ven</sub> t<sub>wo</sub> l<sub>oca</sub>l i<sub>mage pa</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>es � an</sub>d <sub>� ex</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>rom</sub> $I _ { t }$ <sub>an</sub>d $\hat { I } _ { t } ,$ SSIM i<sub>s</sub> d<sub>e</sub>fi<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>as</sub>

$$
\mathrm { S S I M } ( x , y ) = \frac { ( 2 \mu _ { x } \mu _ { y } + C _ { 1 } ) ( 2 \sigma _ { x y } + C _ { 2 } ) } { ( \mu _ { x } ^ { 2 } + \mu _ { y } ^ { 2 } + C _ { 1 } ) ( \sigma _ { x } ^ { 2 } + \sigma _ { y } ^ { 2 } + C _ { 2 } ) } ,\tag{10}
$$

Gray regions correspond to masked i<sub>nva</sub>lid <sub>areas</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>are</sub> <sub>exc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>rom</sub> <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub> It <sub>can</sub> b<sub>e</sub> <sub>o</sub>b<sub>serve</sub>d th<sub>a</sub>t DA<sub>-</sub>2 <sub>ex</sub>hibit<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e</sub> l<sub>arges</sub>t di<sub>screpancy</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> the target frame, likely due to insuficient depth–pose alignment. Specifically, reprojection patterns originating from th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>o</sub>tt<sub>om</sub> <sub>reg</sub>i<sub>on</sub> <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> <sub>anc</sub>h<sub>or</sub> f<sub>rame</sub> <sub>appear</sub> <sub>as</sub> t<sub>wo</sub> <sub>gray</sub> <sub>sem</sub>i<sub>c</sub>i<sub>rcu</sub>l<sub>ar</sub> <sub>reg</sub>i<sub>ons</sub> <sub>on</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th <sub>s</sub>id<sub>es</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>syn</sub>th<sub>es</sub>i<sub>ze</sub>d <sub>v</sub>i<sub>ews,</sub> <sub>w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>are c</sub>l<sub>ear</sub>l<sub>y m</sub>i<sub>ss</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> DA<sub>-</sub>2 <sub>resu</sub>lt<sub>.</sub> D<sub>ep</sub>thC<sub>ra</sub>ft<sub>er ac</sub>hi<sub>eves</sub> b<sub>e</sub>tt<sub>er a</sub>li<sub>gnmen</sub>t b<sub>u</sub>t i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uces severe geome</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub> di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>or</sub>ti<sub>ons, w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>can</sub> b<sub>e a</sub>tt<sub>r</sub>ib<sub>u</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>oma</sub>i<sub>n gap</sub> b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween perspec</sub>ti<sub>ve-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a an</sub>d <sub>panoram</sub>i<sub>c</sub> i<sub>magery.</sub> In contrast, PVDepth produces reprojections that are more geometrically consistent with the target frame. These results highlight the potential of applying PVDepth to downstream tasks that rely on geometrically consistent reprojection, such <sub>as</sub> 3D <sub>recons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on or po</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>-c</sub>l<sub>ou</sub>d<sub>–gu</sub>id<sub>e</sub>d <sub>panoram</sub>i<sub>c wor</sub>ld <sub>genera</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

## F.4 Additional Validation with an Alternative Pose Source

T<sub>o</sub> f<sub>ur</sub>th<sub>er exam</sub>i<sub>ne</sub> th<sub>e ro</sub>b<sub>us</sub>t<sub>ness o</sub>f <sub>our rea</sub>l<sub>-wor</sub>ld <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on an</sub>d <sub>a</sub>dd<sub>ress</sub> th<sub>e concern o</sub>f <sub>po</sub>t<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>a</sub>l <sub>c</sub>i<sub>rcu</sub>l<sub>ar</sub> d<sub>epen</sub>d<sub>ency</sub> <sub>cause</sub>d b<sub>y us</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> ViPE<sub>-</sub>d<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ve</sub>d <sub>poses an</sub>d <sub>sca</sub>l<sub>e a</sub>li<sub>gnmen</sub>t<sub>, we con</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>t <sub>an a</sub>dditi<sub>ona</sub>l <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on w</sub>ith <sub>an a</sub>lt<sub>erna</sub>ti<sub>ve pose</sub> source. S<sub>p</sub>ecificall<sub>y</sub>, we use Ma<sub>p</sub>An<sub>y</sub>thin<sub>g</sub> (Keetha et al., 2025) to estimate camera <sub>p</sub>oses and de<sub>p</sub>th for the same GenEx Real-World clips. Since MapAnything does not directly support ERP input, we first project each panoramic video into a front-view cubemap sequence, estimate depth and camera poses with MapAnything, and then project the estimated d<sub>ep</sub>th b<sub>ac</sub>k t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> ERP d<sub>oma</sub>i<sub>n</sub> t<sub>oge</sub>th<sub>er w</sub>ith th<sub>e correspon</sub>di<sub>ng va</sub>lid<sub>-reg</sub>i<sub>on mas</sub>k<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e resu</sub>lti<sub>ng</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>an</sub>d <sub>poses are</sub> used for scale alignment and reprojection-based evaluation, following the same SSIM metric as in the main real-world <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

Gi<sub>ven</sub> th<sub>e a</sub>b<sub>sence o</sub>f <sub>ex</sub>i<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>ng en</sub>d<sub>-</sub>t<sub>o-en</sub>d <sub>panoram</sub>i<sub>c v</sub>id<sub>eo</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>es</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>ti<sub>on mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s, we compare</sub> PVD<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t th<sub>ree</sub> <sub>ca</sub>t<sub>egor</sub>i<sub>es o</sub>f <sub>approac</sub>h<sub>es</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>can</sub> b<sub>e use</sub>d f<sub>or panoram</sub>i<sub>c v</sub>id<sub>eo</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>es</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>ti<sub>on:</sub> i<sub>mage-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d <sub>panoram</sub>i<sub>c</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s,</sub> <sub>pos</sub>t<sub>-process</sub>i<sub>ng a</sub>li<sub>gnmen</sub>t f<sub>ramewor</sub>k<sub>s, an</sub>d <sub>perspec</sub>ti<sub>ve v</sub>id<sub>eo</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>s.</sub>

Image Depth-based Methods. We included re<sub>p</sub>resentative methods: DA-2 (Li et al., 2025a), UniK3D (Piccinelli et al., 2025), PanoDA (Cao et al., 2025), and De<sub>p</sub>thAn<sub>y</sub>where (Wan<sub>g</sub> & Liu, 2024). Since these al<sub>g</sub>orithms are inherentl<sub>y</sub> d<sub>es</sub>i<sub>gne</sub>d f<sub>or</sub> i<sub>mages,</sub> th<sub>ey canno</sub>t di<sub>rec</sub>tl<sub>y process v</sub>id<sub>eo sequences.</sub> Th<sub>ere</sub>f<sub>ore, we per</sub>f<sub>orm</sub> i<sub>n</sub>f<sub>erence</sub> f<sub>rame-</sub>b<sub>y-</sub>f<sub>rame</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>conca</sub>t<sub>ena</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t<sub>s</sub> t<sub>o</sub> f<sub>orm</sub> th<sub>e v</sub>id<sub>eo</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th<sub>.</sub> T<sub>o</sub> f<sub>ur</sub>th<sub>er ensure a</sub> f<sub>a</sub>i<sub>r compar</sub>i<sub>son, we</sub> h<sub>ave a</sub>l<sub>so</sub> fi<sub>ne-</sub>t<sub>une</sub>d De<sub>p</sub>thAn<sub>y</sub>where (Wan<sub>g</sub> & Liu, 2024) usin<sub>g</sub> its oficial trainin<sub>g</sub> codebase on our dataset.

Post-processing Alignment Methods. These methods (Yan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025b; Huan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025) aim to enforce tem<sub>p</sub>oral <sub>cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ency on</sub> t<sub>op o</sub>f <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e-</sub>f<sub>rame pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> th<sub>roug</sub>h <sub>op</sub>ti<sub>m</sub>i<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on or geome</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c cons</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>s.</sub>

• Matrix-3D (Yan<sub>g</sub> et al.<sub>,</sub> 2025b): This method first <sub>g</sub>enerates sin<sub>g</sub>le-frame <sub>p</sub>anoramic de<sub>p</sub>th ma<sub>p</sub>s b<sub>y</sub> stitchin<sub>g</sub> <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> f<sub>rom a perspec</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> d<sub>ep</sub>th <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>v</sub>i<sub>a</sub> P<sub>o</sub>i<sub>sson</sub> bl<sub>en</sub>di<sub>ng.</sub> S<sub>u</sub>b<sub>sequen</sub>tl<sub>y,</sub> it <sub>en</sub>f<sub>orces</sub> t<sub>empora</sub>l <sub>a</sub>li<sub>gnmen</sub>t through inter-frame reprojection and global least-squares optimization, which explicitly requires known camera <sub>ex</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>i<sub>cs as</sub> i<sub>npu</sub>t<sub>.</sub> N<sub>o</sub>t<sub>e</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t i<sub>n our eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on, we</sub> di<sub>rec</sub>tl<sub>y prov</sub>id<sub>e</sub> M<sub>a</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>x-</sub>3D <sub>w</sub>ith th<sub>e groun</sub>d<sub>-</sub>t<sub>ru</sub>th <sub>camera</sub> <sub>ex</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>i<sub>cs ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t<sub>.</sub> H<sub>owever,</sub> thi<sub>s a</sub>li<sub>gnmen</sub>t <sub>mec</sub>h<sub>an</sub>i<sub>sm s</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>c</sub>tl<sub>y re</sub>li<sub>es on a s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>c scene assump</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> and may break down in the presence of dynamic objects or non-rigid scene changes.

• ViPE (Huan<sub>g</sub> et al.<sub>,</sub> 2025): ViPE ado<sub>p</sub>ts a "<sub>p</sub>redict-then-o<sub>p</sub>timize" <sub>p</sub>aradi<sub>g</sub>m rooted in a dense SLAM framework. It inte<sub>g</sub>rates <sub>p</sub>er-frame de<sub>p</sub>th <sub>p</sub>redictions from UniK3D (Piccinelli et al., 2025) as re<sub>g</sub>ularization terms into a dense Bundle Adjustment (BA) to jointly optimize camera poses and scene geometry. To mitigate interference from moving objects, ViPE employs semantic masks from GroundingDINO and SAM to explicitly exclude dynamic <sub>reg</sub>i<sub>ons</sub> d<sub>ur</sub>i<sub>ng op</sub>ti<sub>m</sub>i<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on.</sub>

Note that HoloTime (Zhou et al., 2025) also attem<sub>p</sub>ts <sub>p</sub>anoramic video de<sub>p</sub>th estimation in <sub>p</sub>i<sub>p</sub>eline, but the<sub>y</sub> fundamentall<sub>y</sub> rely on a static viewpoint assumption (i.e., fixing the background geometry to match the first frame). This constraint <sub>ren</sub>d<sub>ers</sub> th<sub>em</sub> i<sub>ncapa</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> <sub>o</sub>f h<sub>an</sub>dli<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>gn</sub>ifi<sub>can</sub>t 6<sub>-</sub>D<sub>o</sub>F <sub>camera</sub> <sub>mo</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>presen</sub>t i<sub>n</sub> P<sub>ano</sub>CARLA<sub>.</sub> C<sub>onsequen</sub>tl<sub>y,</sub> th<sub>ey</sub> <sub>were</sub> <sub>exc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>d<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>rom</sub> <sub>our</sub> <sub>compar</sub>i<sub>son.</sub>

P<sub>e</sub>r<sub>spec</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> Vid<sub>eo</sub> D<sub>ep</sub>th E<sub>s</sub>tim<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n<sub>.</sub> Thi<sub>s ca</sub>t<sub>egory o</sub>f <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s</sub> i<sub>s capa</sub>bl<sub>e o</sub>f <sub>process</sub>i<sub>ng v</sub>id<sub>eo</sub> i<sub>npu</sub>t<sub>s</sub> i<sub>n an en</sub>d<sub>-</sub>t<sub>o-en</sub>d <sub>manner.</sub> W<sub>e eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d th<sub>em un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> th<sub>ree se</sub>tti<sub>ngs:</sub>

1. Zero-Shot: We evaluated Video De<sub>p</sub>th An<sub>y</sub>thin<sub>g</sub> (Chen et al., 2025), De<sub>p</sub>th An<sub>y</sub> Video (Yan<sub>g</sub> et al., 2025a), and De<sub>p</sub>thCrafter (Hu et al., 2025) b<sub>y</sub> directl<sub>y p</sub>erformin<sub>g</sub> inference on the ERP sequences usin<sub>g</sub> their oficiall<sub>y</sub> released <sub>re-</sub>t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>we</sub>i ht<sub>s.</sub>

2. Fine-Tuned Baseline: To establish a stron<sub>g</sub> baseline, we selected DepthCrafter (Hu et al., 2025) and fine-tuned it <sub>on our</sub> P<sub>ano</sub>CARLA d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t<sub>.</sub> F<sub>or a</sub> f<sub>a</sub>i<sub>r compar</sub>i<sub>son,</sub> thi<sub>s</sub> fi<sub>ne-</sub>t<sub>un</sub>i<sub>ng was con</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>us</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e exac</sub>t <sub>same</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>sc</sub>h<sub>e</sub>d<sub>u</sub>l<sub>e an</sub>d it<sub>era</sub>ti<sub>on coun</sub>t <sub>as our propose</sub>d PVD<sub>ep</sub>th<sub>.</sub>

3. Cube-based Strategy: In this strate<sub>gy</sub> (referred to as De<sub>p</sub>thCrafter (Cube-based) in Tab. 2), the <sub>p</sub>anoramic video is decomposed into six cubemap sequences, which are processed independently and then re-projected back to the ERP f<sub>orma</sub>t<sub>.</sub> W<sub>e eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>t<sub>e</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th th<sub>e zero-s</sub>h<sub>o</sub>t <sub>per</sub>f<sub>ormance o</sub>f D<sub>ep</sub>thC<sub>ra</sub>ft<sub>er un</sub>d<sub>er</sub> thi<sub>s cu</sub>b<sub>emap</sub> f<sub>ormu</sub>l<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on an</sub>d <sub>a</sub> fi<sub>ne-</sub>t<sub>une</sub>d <sub>var</sub>i<sub>an</sub>t t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>ne</sub>d <sub>on</sub> th<sub>e</sub> P<sub>ano</sub>CARLA d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>ase</sub>t<sub>.</sub> D<sub>ur</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> fi<sub>ne-</sub>t<sub>un</sub>i<sub>ng,</sub> i<sub>ns</sub>t<sub>ea</sub>d <sub>o</sub>f di<sub>rec</sub>tl<sub>y us</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> ERP <sub>v</sub>id<sub>eos,</sub> each panoramic video is decomposed into six cubemap videos, which are jointly used as inputs to ensure efective t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng an</sub>d <sub>cross-v</sub>i<sub>ew cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ency.</sub> Vi<sub>sua</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>ons o</sub>f th<sub>e zero-s</sub>h<sub>o</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d fi<sub>ne-</sub>t<sub>une</sub>d <sub>resu</sub>lt<sub>s are s</sub>h<sub>own</sub> i<sub>n</sub> Fi<sub>g.</sub> 10<sub>.</sub>
